             5.9.3 Compressed Request Field
             A Compressed Request may be used for a specific subset of UPLI Requests. If the Request is not in
             this specific subset (see Table 5-33 Compressed Request Command Encoding below), an
             Uncompressed Request shall be used. To issue a compressed Request, the Request shall hit in the
             Tx Address Cache and the entry that hit shall not be removed or invalidated before the Request is
             issued to the Tx TL Flit Channel. A Compressed Request is 2 sectors as opposed to the 4 sectors for
             an Uncompressed Request. This is achieved by encoding the UPLI ReqCmd signal (by only
             supporting a subset of the Commands) and ReqLen signal (by insisting that the address of the
             Request is aligned on a 64 byte boundary and the request is a multiple of 64 bytes in length, and
             does not cross a 256 byte boundary) into smaller signals and omitting the ReqAttr,
             ReqAddr[56:20], and ReqMetaData[7:3] signals. The TL shall not be required to issue a Compressed
             Request and may choose to issue an Uncompressed Request even if conditions would otherwise
             have permitted the issuance of a Compressed Request.
                                                  Table 5-31 Compressed Request Field Signals
              Compressed Request Field (FTYPE = 0x3) signals
              Name                           Size       Position            Description
                                             (bits)
              FTYPE                          4          [63:60]             Field Type. This signal, with a value of 0x3, indicates the
                                                                            Field is a Compressed Request Field.
              CMD                             3         [59:57]             Command. This signal carries a compressed version of the
                                                                            UPLI ReqCmd signal value for this Request. See Table 5-33
                                                                            Compressed Request Command Encoding below for details.
              VCHAN                           2         [56:55]             Virtual Channel. This signal carries the UPLI ReqVC signal
                                                                            value for this Request.
              ASI                             2         [54:53]             Address Space Identifier. This signal carries the UPLI
                                                                            ReqASI signal value for this Request.
              TAG                             11        [52:42]             Tag. This signal carries the UPLI ReqTag signal value for
                                                                            this Request.
              POOL                            1         [41]                Pool. This signal indicates whether the TL is utilizing a Pool
                                                                            Credit to issue this Request or a Virtual Channel Credit. The
                                                                            value on this signal is independent of the ReqPool signal on
                                                                            the UPLI interface (TL Credit management is independent
                                                                            of UPLI Credit Management).
              LEN                             2         [40:39]             Length. This signal carries a compressed version of the
                                                                            UPLI ReqLen signal value for this Request. A value of ‘0’
                                                                            indicates a 64-byte transfer, a value of ‘1’ indicates a 128-
                                                                            byte transfer, a value of ‘2’ indicates a 192 byte transfer,
                                                                            and a value of ‘3’ indicates a 256 byte transfer.
              METADATA                        3         [38:36]             Metadata. This signal carries the UPLI signal
                                                                            ReqMetaData[2:0]. The remaining bits of Metadata are not
                                                                            carried and must have a value of b’00000’.
              ADDR                            14        [35:22]             Address. This signal carries the UPLI ReqAddr[19:6] signal
                                                                            value indicating the aligned 64-byte block within the
                                                                            previously cached 1MB region for this Request that is being
                                                                            addressed. UPLI signal ReqAddr[56:20] are reconstituted
                                                                            from the Rx Address Cache.
              SRCACCID                        10        [21:12]             Source Accelerator ID. This field carries the UPLI
                                                                            ReqSrcPhysAccID signal value for this Request.
              DSTACCID                        10        [11:2]              Destination Accelerator ID. This field carries the UPLI
                                                                            ReqDstPhysAccId signal value for this Request.
              CWAY                            2         [1:0]               Cache Way. Designates which way of the Address Cache to
                                                                            use to reconstitute UPLI signal ReqAddr[56:20] for this
                                                                            Request.
              TOTAL BITS:                     64        2 Sectors

             5.10 Recommended TL backoff modes.
Evaluation Copy
             The following is a list of backoff modes that are recommended to be implemented in a UALink TL:

                        •   A mode to limit the number of Uncompressed Requests that can be packed into a
                            Control Half-Flit to be 1 (at most 2 Uncompressed Requests can be packed into a
                            Control Half-Flit).
                        •   A mode to limit the number of Compressed Requests that can be packed into a
                            Control Half-Flit to be 1, 2, or 3 (at most 4 Compressed Requests can be packed into
                            a Control Half-Flit).
                        •   A mode to limit the number of Uncompressed Responses that can be packed into a
                            Control Half-Flit to be 1, 2, or 3 (at most 4 Uncompressed Responses can be packed
                            in a Control Half-Flit).
                        •   A mode to control the number of Compressed Responses that can be packed into a
                            Control Half-Flit to be 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, or 7 (at most 8 Compressed Responses can be
                            packed into a Control Half-Flit).
                        •   A mode to control the number of non-NOP Flow Control/NOP indications that can
                            be packed into a Control Half-Flit to be 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, or 7 (at most 8 non-NOP Flow
                            Control/NOP Indications can be packed into a Control Half-Flit). It must always be
                            possible to pack 8 NOP Flow Control/NOP indications in a Control Half-Flit to
                            indicate NOP.
                        The above modes are only meant to limit the number of the type of item that may be
                        packed into a Control Half-Flit. The allowed placements of the items within a Control
                        Half-Flit are not affected.

                        •   A mode to disable using the Compressed Address Caches (logically the same as
                            disabling both the TL Tx and TL Rx Address Caches)




                                                     Transaction Layer (TL)                                     147

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             6 UALink In-Network Collectives
Evaluation Copy
             6.1 Introduction
             The workloads on scale-up networks typically do not fit within a single Accelerator and therefore
             must be partitioned across multiple Accelerators. To facilitate the computation and communication
             between Accelerators processing these cross-Accelerator workloads, various Collective
             Communication Libraries such as NCCL, RCCL, MSCCL, MSCCL++ have been created to facilitate
             communication and computation between Accelerators using a concept known as “Collectives”.
             In UALink, a Collective shall be defined as an operation that involves a set of Accelerators, also
             known as a “Group”. Each Accelerator in the Group is also referred to as a “Rank”. These Ranks shall
             have input data buffers and output data buffers (for certain Collectives, the input data buffer or the
             output data buffer may only be present on one “Root Rank”). The input and output buffers may be
             at the same locations in memory but shall not partially overlap (i.e. the input and output buffers
             may be at the same memory locations, or they shall have no overlap).
             A Collective operation reads from the input buffer or buffers in memory on the Accelerators,
             processes the data read through a reduction function if data is read from multiple ranks, and then
             writes to the output buffer or buffers in memory on the Accelerators.




                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                                148

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             6.1.1 BROADCAST Collective
             The BROADCAST Collective is shown below in Figure 6-1:
Evaluation Copy



                                                Figure 6-1 BROADCAST Collective

             The BROADCAST Collective copies an array of “count” elements located at a UALink address
             “sendbuf” from the Root Rank to buffers of “count” elements on all k Ranks in the Collective at a
             UALink address “recvbuf”. If the sendbuf and recvbuf addresses are equal, an “in-place” Collective
             occurs. If the sendbuf and recvbuf addresses are not equal and the input buffer and output buffer
             overlap, the results of the Collective are indeterminate.




                                                 UALink In-Network Collectives                               149

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             6.1.2 REDUCE Collective
             The REDUCE Collective is shown below in Figure 6-2:
Evaluation Copy




                                                   Figure 6-2 REDUCE Collective

             The REDUCE Collective reduces each of the corresponding elements in a given location in sendbuf
             (e.g. A0/A1/A2/A3, B0/B1/B2/B3, etc.) in sendbuf on all the Ranks in the Group using a specified
             Reduction Function “reducef()” to produce a single output value which is placed at the
             corresponding position in the Receive Buffer “recvbuf” on the Root Rank. If the sendbuf and recvbuf
             addresses are equal, an “in-place” Collective occurs. If the sendbuf and recvbuf addresses are not
             equal and the input buffer and output buffer overlap, the results of the Collective are indeterminate.




                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                                150

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             The ALL-REDUCE Collective is shown below in Figure 6-3 :

             6.1.4 REDUCE-SCATTER Collective
             The REDUCE-SCATTER Collective is shown below in Figure 6-4:
Evaluation Copy




                                              Figure 6-4 REDUCE-SCATTER Collective

             The REDUCE-SCATTER Collective reduces repeated arrays of “recvcount*(num_ranks)” elements
             from each input Rank through a reduction function (redcuef()) to produce a set of num_ranks
             arrays of size revcount (each labeled box, A0-A3, B0-B3, C0-C3, D0-D3, or A, B, C, and D, above
             represents an array of elements rather than a single element).
             The 1st array produced (A0-A3 above) is placed in Rank0’s memory at the recvbuf address for
             Rank0, the 2nd array produced (B0-B3 above) is placed in Rank1’s memory at the specified recvbuf
             address and so on for the remaining Ranks.
             If recvbuf = sendbuf+(rank*recvcount) for all Ranks, an “in-place” Collective occurs. If the sendbuf
             and recvbuf addresses do not follow this rule, and the sendbuf buffer and the recvbuf buffers
             overlap, the results of the Collective are indeterminate.




                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                                152

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             A series of repeated REDUCE Collectives can be utilized to implement REDUCE-SCATTER as shown
             below in Figure 6-5:

             6.2 Collective Primitives
             UALink provides Collective Primitives as a mechanism to help implement Collectives. Collective
Evaluation Copy
             Primitive Requests, ReadReduce, WriteMulticast, WriteFullMulticast, and AtomicNRMulticast, are
             variants of the Read, Write, WriteFull, and AtomicNR Requests, respectively, that UALink uses for
             unicast Requests. Sequences of read and write accesses to local memory on the Accelerators along
             with Collective Primitive Requests can be used to implement Collectives.
             Collective Primitive Requests are issued by an Accelerator, replicated at the Switch, and then issued
             to the Accelerators in the Colective Group. When issued from the Requesting Accelerator, each
             Collective Primitive Request shall have an associated “GroupID” value (which shall be conveyed in
             ReqDstPhysAccID signal) that shall be used to index a Group Table on the Switch that shall indicate
             the members of the Collective Group. This Group Table shall be pre-loaded when a Collective Group
             is created and before any Collective Primitive Requests with that GroupID are issued.
             A ReadReduce Request issued from an Accelerator also has additional indications of the Data Type
             of the data being read, the Reduction Operation to be performed on the data being read, the
             precision of the reduction if applicable and the Rounding Mode to be used if applicable (all of which
             shall be conveyed in the ReqSrcPhysAccID signal). The Switch shall look up the Group members and
             shall issue a ReadReduce operation to each Accelerator in the Group.
             The Destnation Accelerators shall separately return a Read Response Status indication and
             Response Data for the individual ReadReduce Requests. The Switch shall reduce the individual
             copies of the Response Data into a single copy of data according to the specified Reduction
             Operation, Reduction Precision (if applicable), DataType, and Rounding mode (if applicable). The
             Switch shall also reduce the Read Response Status indications received from each of the
             Accelerators in the Group. The Switch shall then return the reduced single copy of the Response
             Data and Response Status to the Requesting Accelerator that initially issued the ReadReduce
             Request.
             WriteMulticast, WriteFullMulticast, and AtomicNRMulticast Requests shall follow a similar
             progression. The Requesting Accelerator shall issue the WriteMulticast, WriteFullMulticast, or
             AtomicNRMulticast Request with write data or operand data, respectively, to the Switch. The
             Switch shall look up the Group members and shall issue a WriteMulticast, WriteFullMulticast, or
             AtomicNRMulticast Request, respectively, to each Accelerator in the Group. Each destination
             Accelerator shall update memory and shall return a Write Response status indication. The
             WriteMulticast, WriteFullMulticast, and AtomicNRMulticast Collective Primitives shall update
             memory in the same manner as a Write, WriteFull, or AtomicNR Request, respectively. The Switch
             shall combine the returned Write Response Status indications into a single overall Write Response
             Status indication and shall return this Write Response Status indication to the Requesting
             Accelerator.
             Collective Primitives issued from a given Accelerator typically use the same mechanism to select an
             egress port on the Accelerator as unicast Requests to spread the Collective Primitives Requests
             across the Pod Switches in the same manner as unicast Requests.
             As with the AtomicNR operations in UALink, the exact nature of the atomic read-modify-write
             operation performed by an AtomicNRMulticast is implementation specific.




                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                               154

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                    Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                        Rev 2.0

             6.2.1 Switch Structures to support Collective Primitives
             To properly support the replication of the initial Collective Primitive sent by the Accelerator to the
Evaluation Copy
             Switch, the Switch shall implement various additional tables and structures.
                        1. Each Port shall contain a Group Table consisting of up to 1024 entries. Each entry in
                           this table shall consist of a valid bit and a bitmask of a width equal to the radix of the
                           Switch. The index of each bit in the Group Table entry shall correspond to a Physical
                           Accelerator ID for an Accelerator within the Collective Group (i.e. Accelerator ID
                           numbers shall be assigned within the range 0 to the radix of the switch minus 1).
                           The Group Table shall be programmable by privileged system software.
                        2. Each Switch Port shall contain a 10-bit register to identify the Accelerator ID of the
                           Accelerator attached to the Port. These registers shall be programmed by the Pod
                           Controller.
             For Collective Primitive Requests, the ReqDstPhysAccID field shall contain a GroupID[9:0] value
             that specifies the entry in the Group Table associated with the Collective Primitive. This GroupID
             value shall be present on both the Request issued by the Requesting Accelerator and on the
             duplicated Requests issued by the Switch to the Destination Accelerators.
             The ReqSrcPhysAccID field shall be overloaded between the Requesting Accelerator and the Switch
             with a ReduceOpDataType[6:0] value in bits [6:0] and by a RoundingMode[2:0] value in bits [9:7].
             The ReduceOpDataType value indicates the Reduction Operation, the DataType of the data, the
             Reduction precision if necessary, and the write back Data Type if necessary.
             For a ReadReduce Collective Primitive, the Switch shall set the ReqSrcPhysAccID field on the
             replicated ReadReduce Requests to the Requesting Accelerator ID. This shall be accomplished by
             referencing a 10-bit register at the given Ingress Port that indicates the Accelerator ID for the
             Accelerator attached to that Port. This register shall be programmed by the Pod Controller.
             If the Collective Primitive is not a ReadReduce, The ReqSrcPhysAccID field shall contain the
             Accelerator ID of the Requesting Accelerator on both the initial Request from the Requesting
             Accelerator and on the replicated Requests to the Destination Accelerators in the Group.




                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                                  155

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                    Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                        Rev 2.0

             The structures to Support a Collective Primitive and the progression of a Collective Primitive
             through these structures are illustrated below in Figure 6-6:
Evaluation Copy


                                          Figure 6-6 Collective Primitives Support Structures

             A Collective Primitive Request is Received by the Switch at . The GroupID[9:0] value in
             ReqDstPhysAccID shall be used to index the GroupTable to obtain the bitmask entry in the
             GroupTable (the Group Accelerator Mask) and the Valid bit. This bitmask shall indicate the
             Accelerators in the Group in terms of Accelerator Physical IDs (i.e. bit [5] = b’1’ implies the
             Accelerator with Physical ID 5 is in the group) .
             If the Valid bit is not set, or the bit corresponding to the Requesting Accelerator is not set in the
             Group Accelerator Mask, the Switch shall drop the Collective Primitive Request and shall issue a
             TARGET ABORT Response to the Switch for the Collective Primitive and return a fixed data pattern
             for ReadReduce. If the Group Accelerator Mask has less than two bits set, the Accelerator shall drop
             the Collective Primitive Request and shall issue a TARGET ABORT Response to the Switch. The
             Switch may begin processing the Collective Primitive before recognizing this error condition. If the
             ReqLen, ReqAddr, and ReqAttr fields for a Read Reduce Collective Primitive do not indicate a region
             of memory that is aligned to start at and is a non-zero integer multiple of the size of the output data
             elements being returned to the Switch, the Switch shall drop the Collective Primitive Request and
             shall issue a TARGET ABORT response to the Switch. If the values for either
             ReduceOpDataType[6:0] or RoundingMode[2:0] conveyed in ReqSrcPhysAccID are not one of the
             legal values, the Accelerator shall drop the Collective Primitive Request and shall issue a TARGET
             ABORT Response to the Switch.
             Otherwise, the Group Accelerator Mask, shall be passed on to the Primitives INC engine  along
             with the Request and the Physical Accelerator ID of the Requesting Accelerator  for processing.
             The Primitives INC Engine shall issue a Request to Switch Route Table for each Physical Accelerator
             ID indicated in the Group Accelerator Mask . The Switch Route Table (which is also used for non-
             INC Requests)  shall provide the Destination Port Identifier for the replicated Request instance
             and the replicated Request instance shall be routed to the Destination Port and issued to the
             Destination Accelerator .

             Isolation between Virtual Pods for Collective Primitives shall be provided by the Switch Route Table
             in the same manner as for non-INC Requests.

             6.2.2 Collective Primitive Request/Response Flows
             The following figure, Figure 6-7, illustrates the flow of the Collective Primitive Requests:

             The Group Table entry for the Block Collective shall be loaded prior to the initiation of the
             Collective Primitive . A Collective Primitive Request is initiated by Accelerator “A”  and is
             transmitted to the Collectives Primitive Engine. The ReqDstPhysAccID field shall be overlaid with
             the GroupID[9:0] value for the Collective Primitive. The ReqSrcPhysAccID field shall be overlaid
             with the ReduceOpDataType[6:0] in ReqSrcPhysAccID[2:0] and with the RoundMode[2:0] value in
             ReqSrcPhysAccID[9:7] for a ReadReduce Collective Primitive and shall indicate the Requesting
             Accelerator Physical Accelerator ID for all other Collective Primitives. For a ReadReduce Request,
             without regard to the RoundMode value, the ReqAttr field shall contain an 8-bit Stochastic Seed
             value that shall be used when the Rounding Mode is Stochastic.

             GroupID[9:0] value to obtain the Group Accelerator Mask . The Collectives Primitive Engine shall

             with the ReqSrcPhysAccID field set to indicate the Physical Accelerator ID of the Requesting
             Accelerator . The following signals in the Request Channel shall be passed through the Switch
             unchanged from the initial Request to each of the duplicated Requests: ReqASI, ReqTag,
             ReqNumBeats, ReqAddr, ReqCmd, ReqLen, ReqMetaData, ReqVC. If OrigDataError is asserted for

             the Destination Accelerators.
             The Collectives Primitive Engine shall maintain tracking logic to track the outstanding Requests for
             each Collective Primitive, to reduce the Responses for the outstanding Requests for each Collective

             the Collective Primitive have been received and reduced.
             When an individual Request is received at the Destination Accelerator Completer, the Completer
             shall retain indications for the type of Request received  in order to properly drive the
             RdRspTypeInfo[1:0] and WrRspTypeInfo[1:0] signals.
             The Destination Accelerator shall distinguish tags according to a combination of the ReqTag value,
             the ReqSrcPhysAccID value, the ReqPortID value, and whether the Request is a BlockWrite,
             BlockWriteFull, or BlockRead Request or the Request is any other Request Type . This tracking
             scheme provides Block* Requests for Block Collectives (whose tags are assigned by the Switch
             issuing the Requests) an independent tag space from the tag space for the Collective Primitives and
             normal Requests (whose tags are assigned by the Accelerator issuing the Requests) preventing tag
             collisions between the two groups of Requests.

             6.3 Block Collectives
             UALink provides Block Collectives as an alternative means to completely offload BROADCAST,
Evaluation Copy
             REDUCE, and ALL-REDUCE Collectives to the Switch for processing. In a Block Collective, all
             accesses to memory to perform the Collective are instigated by the Switch. Block Collectives do not
             include the ALL-GATHER and REDUCE-SCATTER Collectives. For ALL-GATHER the Input Buffer at
             each Rank may have a unique address and similarly for the Output Buffer for each Rank for
             REDUCE-SCATTER. It is impractical to implement these Collectives as a Block Collective because a
             list of addresses for each rank would have to conveyed to the Switch for each Block Collective (up to
             1024 57-bit addresses).
             The Input and Output Buffer addresses for Block Collectives shall start on a 256-byte address
             boundary and have a transfer length that is a multiple of 256 bytes. To initiate a Block Collective,
             the Requesting Accelerator shall issue a BlockCollectiveInvoke Request which is a write operation
             that delivers a 64-byte data payload to the Switch. The 64-byte data payload shall contain a Block
             Collective Control Block. The Block Collective Control Block shall contain the parameters necessary
             to specify the Collective.
             When a BlockCollectiveInvoke Request arrives at a Switch, the Switch shall always return an
             “OKAY” Write Response Status indication without undue delay. The Switch shall then, subject to
             various error checks (described below Table 6-4), enqueue the Block Collective in a Block Collective
             Control Block Queue (Figure 6-9) at the Port that received the BlockCollectiveInvoke. If an error
             occurred when enqueuing the Block Collective, the Switch shall drop the Block Collective and
             indicate an error by updating the Block Collective Status Buffer (Figure 6-16).
             Once a Block Collective has been enqueued in the Switch, it shall eventually be dequeued, according
             to an implementation specific policy, to be processed. To distinguish between Requests for Block
             Collectives and Requests for unicast Requests and Collective Primitives, the Switch shall issue a
             series of BlockRead and BlockWriteFull Requests for all reads to memory and all updates to
             memory (including updates to the Status Buffer), respectively, due to a Block Collective. The Tag
             values for these BlockRead and BlockWriteFull Requests shall be generated by the Switch and shall
             be independent of (i.e. the values may overlap) the tag values used for unicast Requests and
             Collective Primitive Requests.
             For a given 256-byte block in a Collective, the Switch shall perform Block Read(s) to the Input
             Buffer(s), shall then perform the reduction on the read data if applicable, and then shall perform
             BlockWriteFull(s) to the Output Buffer(s). The order of the Block Read(s) for a given 256-byte block
             to the various Ranks in the Collective shall be implementation dependent. The order of the Block
             Write(s) for a given 256-byte block to the various Rank(s) in the Collective shall be implementation
             dependent.
             For any reduction operation that is not associative, the reduction for a given 256-byte block shall
             occur in an implementation specific order that shall remain the same for every Block Collective
             instance and shall be determined solely by the set of Ranks involved in the Collective. The Block
             Read Requests and Block Write Requests for a given Block Collective across multiple Collectives
             may be freely interleaved in an implementation specific fashion subject to the above constraints for
             Requests for a given 256-byte block within the Collective.
             If a BlockWriteFull or a BlockRead that accesses the input buffer(s) or output buffer(s) receives a
             Response Status indication other than “OKAY”, the Switch shall cease issuing new BlockWriteFull
             and BlockRead Requests for the Block Collective and shall wait for any outstanding BlockWriteFull
             or BlockRead Reuqests to return their Responses. The Block Collective shall then be dropped and

                                                 UALink In-Network Collectives                                161

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                    Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                        Rev 2.0

             the Switch shall write the Status Buffer using a BlockWriteFull to indicate the failure of the Block
             Collective.
Evaluation Copy
             If the Block Collective completes successfully, the Switch shall update the Status Buffer using a
             BlockWriteFull to indicate the successful completion of the Block Collective,
             While Block Collectives directly implement the full semantics of a Collective and therefore repeated
             invocations of a Block Collective are not required to perform a Collective, it may still be useful to
             shard (divide) the input and output arrays in the Collective into distinct subsets that are processed
             by different Accelerators in the Collective.
             Furthermore, it may be useful to further divide the subset of the Collective assigned to a given
             Accelerator into smaller distinct subsets that are processed by different Block Collectives that are
             issued to the different Switches in the Pod. The Switch in the Pod that processes a Block Collective
             invocation is determined by the Egress Port the Accelerator issues the BlockCollectiveInvoke
             Request on.
             Unlike Collective Primitives, which are typically issued on an Egress Port derived from the address
             of the Collective Primitive, the Egress Port of a BlockCollectiveInvoke Request is typically controlled
             by an Accelerator specific means, usually software driven, that allows the spreading of the Block
             Collective invocations for a given Accelerator to the various Switches in the Pod.
             6.3.1 Switch Block Collective Queues
             A Switch shall contain a per-Port Control Block Queue with an implementation specific number of
             entries. Each entry in the Control Block Queue shall contain a Valid bit, the Submission Queue
             Number from the InvokeBlokcCollective Request, the fields associated with the
             InvokeBlockCollective Request as needed (e.g. ReqAddr, ReqSrcPhysAccID, ReqDstPhysAccID,
             ReqNumBeats, ReqTag, ReqVC, etc.), and the parameters that were contained in the data delivered
             with the InvokeBlockCollective Request. Each Port shall also contain a set of Control Logic for
             managing the Control Block Queue entries as shown below in Figure 6-9:




                                          Figure 6-9 Control Block Queue and Control Logic

             The Control Block Queue entries shall be dynamically utilized by various numbered logical
             “Submission Queues” consisting of one or more of the Control Block Queue entries. The set of


                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                                  162

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             Control Block Queue entries assigned to a given Submission Queue may change within the lifetime
             of a given Submission Queue being active.
Evaluation Copy
             Submission Queue numbers shall fall in a range between 0 to 63 inclusive. For example, a Control
             Block Queue containing 64 entries could have 16 entries to Submission Queue 0, 8 entries to
             Submission Queue 1, 8 entries to Submission Queue 2, and 32 entries to submission Queue 15 with
             the rest being reassigned or any of several other combinations.
             The logical Submission Queues within the Control Block Queue at a Port are utilized to provide flow
             control amongst Block Collectives received at a Port
             6.3.2 BlockCollectiveAllocate Request
             To allocate a set of Control Block Queue entries to a given Submission Queue, the Accelerator shall
             issue a BlockCollectiveAllocate Request that specifies in the ReqAddr field the Submission Queue
             and the number of entries being requested for allocation as shown in the following figure (Figure
             6-10):




                                    Figure 6-10 ReqAddr field for BlockColectiveAllocate Request

             The Submission Queue number that the entries in the Collective Block Control Queue shall be
             allocated to shall be indicated in bits [21:16] of ReqAddr allowing for Submission Queue numbers
             of 0 to 63 inclusive. The number of Queue entries being requested for the given Submission Queue
             shall be indicated in bits [28:22] of ReqAddr, allowing for 1 to 128 queues being requested inclusive
             (bits [28:22] shall indicate the number of queues being requested minus 1).
             The low order eight bytes of the 64-bytes of data delivered with the AllocateBlockCollective
             Request shall indicate the 256-byte aligned UALink Address of the Status Buffer for the
             BlockCollectiveAllocate Request as shown in the following figure (Figure 6-11):



                             Figure 6-11 Low Order 8 bytes of Data Conveyed with BlockCollectiveAllocate

             The remaining bytes within the 64 bytes of data delivered with the AllocateBlockCollective Request
             are Reserved and shall be set to zero. The Status Buffer Address shall be StatusBufferAddress[56:8]
             concatenated with eight bits of b’0’ to produce a 256-byte aligned address that shall point to a 256-
             byte buffer that the Switch shall update using a BlockWriteFull Request to indicate the status of the
             BlockColleciveAllocate Request. The first byte at this 256-byte buffer address shall indicate the
             status.
             The high-order bit of the status byte shall be a Valid indication that indicates the Switch has
             updated the status. Software shall ensure that the Valid bit is cleared in the status byte before
             issuing the associated BlockCollectiveAllocate Reqest and shall poll the status byte after issuing the
             BlockCollectiveAllocate Request until the Valid is set by the Switch after the BlockCollectiveAllocate
             Request is issued.

             shown in the following Table (Table 6-2):

             If a Switch receives a BlockCollectiveAllocate Request for a Submission Queue that is currently
             allocated, the Switch will drop the BlockCollectiveAllocate Request and update the status buffer to
             indicate “Duplicate Allocation” (x’02’). If a Switch receives a BlockCollectiveAllocate Request for a
             Submission Queue that is currently unallocated, but is also requesting more entries than remain
             unallocated in the Control Block Queue or a Submission Queue that is currently being allocated or
             deallocated, the Switch will drop the BlockCollectiveAllocate Request and update the status buffer
             to indicate “Invalid Allocation” (x’01’).
             If, however, the Switch receives a BlockCollectiveAllocate Request for an unallocated Submission
             Queue and there are sufficient unallocated entries to fulfill the Request, the Switch shall allocate the
             entires to the requested Submission Queue and update the status buffer to indicate “Successful
             Allocation” (x’00’).
             6.3.3 BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request
             To deallocate the Control Block Queue entries currently allocated to a given Submission Queue or to
             simply ensure that the operations associated with the Submission Queue are completed, the
             Accelerator shall issue a BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request that specifies the Submission Queue
             being deallocated or drained in the ReqAddr field as shown in the following figure (Figure 6-12):



                                    Figure 6-12 ReqAddr field for BlockColectiveDeallocate Request

             The Submission Queue being deallocated shall be indicated in bits [21:16] of ReqAddr allowing for
             Submission Queue numbers of 0 to 63 inclusive. The “DO” (drain only) bit [22] shall indicate if
             entries associated with the SQ are to be deallocated after the outstanding operations complete or
             are terminated (DO = b’0’) or if the entries are to remain allocated (DO = b’1’).
             The low order eight bytes of the 64-bytes of data delivered with the DeallocateBlockCollective
             Request shall indicate the 256-byte aligned UALink Address of the 256-byte Status Buffer for the
             BlockCollectiveAllocate Request as shown in the following figure (Figure 6-13):



                            Figure 6-13 Low Order 8 bytes of Data Conveyed With CollectiveBlockDeallocate

             The remaining bytes within the 64 bytes of data delivered with the DeallocateBlockCollective
             Request are Reserved and shall be set to zero. The Status Buffer Address shall be
                                                    UALink In-Network Collectives                                164

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                    Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                        Rev 2.0

             StatusBufferAddress[56:8] concatenated with eight bits of b’0’ to produce a 256-byte aligned
             address that points to a 256-byte buffer that the Switch shall update using a BlockWriteFull
Evaluation Copy
             Request. The first byte at this 256-byte buffer address shall indicate the status. The remaining bytes
             in the buffer are Reserved and shall be written to zero.
             The high-order bit of the status byte shall be a Valid indication that indicates the Switch has
             updated the status. Software shall ensure that the Valid bit is cleared in the status byte before
             issuing the associated BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request and shall poll the status byte after issuing
             the BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request until the Valid is set by the Switch after the
             BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request is issued.
             The status response encoding for bits [6:0] for the status byte for a BlockCollectiveAllocate
             command are shown in the following Table (Table 6-3):

                                             Status[6:0] Meaning
                                             x’00’            Successful Deallocation
                                             x’01’            Invalid Deallocation
                                             x’02’-x’FF’      Reserved.


             If a Switch receives a BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request for a Submission Queue that is not
             currently allocated or is currently being allocated or deallocated, the Switch shall drop the
             BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request and update the status buffer to indicate “Invalid Deallocation”
             (x’01’).
             If a Switch receives a BlockCollectiveDeallocate Request for a Submission Queue that is currently
             allocated and active, the Switch shall stop accepting any new BlockCollectiveInvoke Requests for
             that Submission Queue. The Switch shall stop issuing new BlockRead and BlockWriteFull Requests
             for any outstanding Block Collectives in the Submission Queue and shall allow the outstanding
             BlockRead and BlockWrite Requets to complete. When the BlockRead and BlockWrite Requests for
             a given Block Collective have Completed or the given Block Collective was terminated before
             starting, the Switch shall update the status buffer for the Block Collective. When the status buffer(s)
             have been updated for all outstanding Block Collectives, the Switch shall update the status byte for
             the BlockCollectiveDeallocate to indicate “Successful Deallocation” (x’00’) and once that update is
             complete, the Submission Queue shall be considered unallocated and is available to be reallocated.
             6.3.4 BlockCollectiveInvoke Request
             To invoke a Block Collective, the Accelerator shall issue a BlockCollectiveInvoke Request that
             specifies the Submission Queue to process the Request in bits [21:16] of ReqAddr and a “Base
             Address” in bits [56:22] of ReqAddr as shown in the following figure (Figure 6-14):




                                     Figure 6-14 ReqAddr field for BlockColectiveInvoke Request

             The “Base Address” of the Block Collective shall be the address given by the ReqAddr field of the
             BlockCollectiveInvoke Request with the low-order 22 bits zeroed as show in the following figure
             (Figure 6-15):

                                                     UALink In-Network Collectives                               165

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                    Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
Evaluation Copy                                         Rev 2.0




                                              Figure 6-15 Base Address of the Block Collective

             The 64 bytes of data delivered with the BlockCollectiveInvoke shall contain a set of parameters to
             fully specify the Collective to be performed by the Switch (Section 6.3.7). One of these parameters
             shall be a 48-bit offset value to which eight bits of zero are concatenated to form an offset that is
             added to the Base Address to compute the 256-byte aligned address of the 256-byte Status Buffer.
             The Switch shall use a BlockWriteFull Request to update the 256-byte status buffer to indicate the
             completion status of the Block Collective.
             The low-order 16 bytes of the 256-byte Status Buffer shall contain a status field for the Block
             Collective. The remaining bytes in the 256-byte Status Buffer are reserved and shall be written to
             zero. The lower-order 16 bytes of the Status Buffer are illustrated in the following figure (Figure
             6-16):




                                 Figure 6-16 Low Order 16 bytes of BlockCollectiveInvoke Status Buffer

             The fields in the Status Buffer are described in the following table (Table 6-4):
                                    Table 6-4 Block Collective Invoke Status Buffer Field Definitions
                    Name                  Size                                          Description
                    V0                    1         Valid0: This field indicates that the IStatus field is valid. The V0 and V1 bits are
                                                    set independently and both may be active.
                    V1                    1         Valid1: This field indicates that the W, BStatus, AccID, and Fail Address fields
                                                    are valid. The V0 and V1 bits are set independently and both may be active.
                    IStatus               4         Invocation/Termination Status: This field indicates the nature of the Invocation
                                                    Error for the Block Collective or that the Block Collective was terminated for a
                                                    BlockCollectiveDeallocate.
                    W                     1         Block Read/Block Write: This field indicates the error occurred on a BLockRead
                                                    (W=0) or a BlockWrite (W=1).
                    BStatus               10        Block Read/Block Write Status: This field indicates the RdRspStatus field value
                                                    for the Response (if W=0) for the error and indicates the WrRspStatus field
                                                    values for the Write Response (if W=1) for the error.
                    AccID                 11        Acccelerator Physical ID: This field indicates the Physical Acceelrator ID of the
                                                    Accelerator that the error occurred on.
                    FailAddres            57        Fail Address: This field indicates the 256-byte aligned address that the failure
                                                    occurred at.


             The V0 and V1 bits in the Status Buffer shall each be a Valid indication that indicates the Switch has
             updated the status. Software shall ensure that both bits are cleared before issuing the
             BlockCollectiveInvoke Request and shall poll these bits until at least one is set by the Switch after
             the BlockCollectiveInvoke Request is issued.
             The information fields (W, Bstatus, AccID and Fail Address) should be set on a best-effort basis to
             ensure that the Bstatus, AccID, W, and Fail Address fields are consistent. For example, if a first error
             is detected on a given BlockWrite for a given Block Collective for some 256-byte region of memory,
             the Switch may present only the information associated with that initial error, even if the IStatus for

                                                      UALink In-Network Collectives                                                        166

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             the Block Collective later changes to INC-MixedResponses as subsequent outstanding Requests are
             received (or the Switch may choose to present the INC-MixedResponses). If an initial error occurs
Evaluation Copy
             for a given address, the Switch may report that first address or some later Request address.
             If a Block Collective is terminated by a BlockCollectiveDeallocate Operation while completing the
             Block Collective after an error is detected, both V0 and V1 may be set or the Switch may choose to
             only track and report one of the errors.
             The status response encoding for Istatus[3:0] field is shown in the following Table (Table 6-5):
                                      Table 6-5 BlockCollectiveAllocate IStatus Field Indications

                                              Status[3:0] Meaning
                                              x’0’            Successful Invocation
                                              x’1’            Invalid Invocation
                                              x’2’            Insufficient Resources
                                              x’3’            Terminated
                                              x’4’-x’F’       Reserved.


             If the Switch receives a BlockCollectiveInvoke Request and the Valid bit for the corresponding
             Group Table entry is not set or the bit in the Group Table Mask corresponding to the Requesting
             Accelerator is not set, the Switch shall drop the BlockCollectiveInvoke Request and update the
             Status Buffer to indicate “Invalid Invocation” (x’1’).
             If the Group Accelerator Mask has less than two bits set, the Accelerator shall drop the
             BlockCollectiveInvoke Request and shall and update the Status Buffer to indicate “Invalid
             Invocation” (x’1’). The Switch may begin processing the Block Collective before recognizing this
             error condition. If the values in any of the BTYPE, CTYPE, ROPDT, RMODE fields in Control Block
             delivered with the BlockCollectiveInvoke contain an illegal value, the Accelerator shall drop the
             BlockCollectiveInvoke Request and shall update the Status Buffer to indicate “Invalid Invocation”
             (x’1’). If the ReqLen and ReqAddr fields for the Block Collective do not indicate a 64-byte aligned 64
             byte region of memory, the Swtich shall drop the BlockCollectiveInvoke Requst and shall update the
             Status Buffer to indicate “Invalid Invocation” (x’1’).


             If the Switch receives a BlockCollectiveInvoke Request and the indicated Submission Queue is not
             active or is being deallocated, the Switch shall drop the BlockCollectiveInvoke Request and update
             the Status Buffer to indicate “Invalid Invocation” (x’1’). If the Switch receives a
             BlockCollectiveAllocate Request and the indicated Submission Queue is active, but full, the Switch
             shall drop the BlockCollectiveRequest and update the Status Buffer to indicate “Insufficient
             Resources” (x’2’).
             If the Switch receives a BlockCollectiveInvoke Request for an active and not full Submission Queue
             and the Group Table entry is valid and the bit in the Group Table Maks corresponding to the
             Requesting Accelerator is set, the Switch shall enqueue the Block Collective for processing and shall
             set the Istatus field to “Successful Invocation” (x’0’).

             6.3.5 Block Collective Normal and Strided Accesses/Address Masking
             For Block Collectives, an Offset parameter (be it for the Input or Output Buffer(s) at the Ranks) shall
Evaluation Copy
             be concatenated with eight binary 0’s and then added to the Base Address for the Block Collective
             to determine the starting 256-byte aligned address for the Buffer or Tile to be accessed.
             For non-strided accesses Block Collective, a Length parameter indicates the number of 256-byte
             blocks of memory at the starting address that should be processed (Length+1 blocs) for the Block
             Collective as shown in the following figure (Figure 6-17):




                                   Figure 6-17 Non-Strided Block Collective Memory Access Pattern

             For a strided access Block Collective, an Xsize, Ysize, and a Skipsize parameter shall be used to
             indicate how many 256-byte blocks (Xsize+1 blocks) shall be processed in a row of a Tile before
             skipping Skipsize+1 256-byte blocks to reach the beginning of the next row of the Tile. The process
             for a strided Block Collective shall repeat for Ysize+1 rows as shown in the following figure (Figure
             6-18):




                                     Figure 6-18 Strided Block Collective Memory Access Pattern

             For non-strided access Block Collectives, the Xsize, Ysize, and Skipsize parameters are invalid and
             shall be ignored. For strided access Block Collectives, the Length parameter is invalid and shall be
             ignored.
             6.3.6 Address Masking
             The Switch shall support one logical Block Collective Address Mask Register that may be used to
             provide memory isolation for Block Collective memory accesses. The Register shall consist of bits
             corresponding to ReqAddr[56:24] as shown in the following figure (Figure 6-19):



                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                                 168

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
Evaluation Copy                                        Rev 2.0




                                         Figure 6-19 Block Collective Address Mask Register

             When used, the Block Collective Address Mask Register shall be loaded with a mask with a
             contiguous set of one or more b’1’ values starting at bit 56.
             When the Switch generates an address for a BlockWrite or BlockRead, the Switch shall invert the
             mask and logically AND the inverted mask with the generated BlockWrite or BlockRead address to
             produce an Address with the high-order bits set to zero according to the mask. The Switch shall also
             bitwise AND the un-inverted mask with the Base Address for the Block Collective producing an
             address containing only the high-order bits of the Base Address. The Switch shall then logically OR
             these two addresses together to form the address that shall be used for the Block Collective.
             This process has the effect preventing the Switch from generating a BlockRead or BlockWrite
             address outside a region bounded by the low-order bits of ReqAddr that are not masked off and is
             identified by the high-order bits of ReqAddr that are masked off by the Block Collective Address
             Mask.

             6.3.8 Switch Structures to support Block Collectives
             The Switch shall use the same Group Table and Switch Route Table structures used for Collective
Evaluation Copy
             Primitives to determine the Accelerators involved in a Block Collective and to route Block*
             Requests that effect the Block Collective as illustrated below in Figure 6-20.




                                          Figure 6-20 Block Collectives Support Structures

             The Block Collective Request is Received at the Group Table from the Control Block Queue at .
             The ReqDstPhysAccID signal value stored in the Block Collective shall contain the GroupID[9:0]
             value which shall be used to index the GroupTable to obtain the Group Accelerator Mask
             corresponding to the Block Collective .
             The Group Accelerator Mask shall be passed on to the Block INC engine  unless the entry is Group
             Table entry is not valid or the bit in the Group Table mask corresponding to the Requesting
             Accelerator for the Block Collective is not set, in which case the Switch shall terminate the Block
             Collective and write the Status Buffer Istatus field with an Invalid Invocation indication. The
             ReqSrcPhysAccID field in the Request shall be reconstituted by referencing the 10-bit register
             containing the Accelerator ID for the Accelerator connected to the Port and the Request is then
             passed to the Block INC Engine for processing .
             The Block INC Engine shall issue a set of Block* Requests to the Switch Route Table to effect the
             Block Collective . The Switch Route Table (which is also used for non-INC Requests)  shall
             provide the Destination Port Identifier for the various Block* Requests that shall be routed to the
             appropriate Destination Port and issued to the Destination Accelerator. .
             Isolation between Virtual Pods for Block Collective Block* Requests shall be provided by the Switch
             Route Table in the same manner as for non-INC Requests.

             BlockRead), or neither of the above .

             6.4 Relationships between the Collectives, Collective Primitives, and
                 Block Collectives
Evaluation Copy
             There is a hierarchical relationship between the Collective Primitives and the Block Collectives.
             Specifically, a properly structured sequence of Collective Primitives can be used to implement all
             the Block Collectives. A sequence of WriteFullMulticasts (that either read from local memory on the
             root rank or read from registers loaded from local memory on the root rank) can be used to
             implement a BROADCAST Collective and a sequence of ReadReduce Collective Primitives (that
             either update local memory on the Root Rank or update registers that are then written back to local
             memory on the Root Rank by store instructions) can be used to implement a REDUCE Collective.
             The BROADCAST and REDUCE Collectives thus implemented can then be used to implement ALL-
             REDUCE, ALL-GATHER, REDUCE-SCATTER.
             UALink supports Block Collectives that directly implement BROADCAST, REDUCE, and ALL-
             REDUCE. The ALL-GATHER and REDUCE-SCATTER Collectives can be implemented using repeated
             BROADCAST and REDUCE Block Collectives Respectively. In addition, the ALL-REDUCE Collective,
             can be implemented by a REDUCE Block Collective followed by a BROADCAST Block Collective.
             The AtomicNR-Multicast Collective Primitive is not typically intended to implement a Collective.
             Rather, the AtomicNR-Multicast is intended to provide Accelerators with a straightforward means
             of updating synchronization variables in the local memory of each Rank. For example, each Rank
             could issue an AtomicNRMulticast to increment a copy of a counter at each Rank when that Rank
             has completed its work on the Collective (presuming that the implementation AtomicNRMulticast
             has an increment or add function). The individual Ranks can then poll the local copy of the
             synchronization variable to determine when all Ranks have completed their processing.




                                                 UALink In-Network Collectives                              179

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                      Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                          Rev 2.0

             These relationships between the Collective Primitives, Block Collectives, and the various Collectives
             are illustrated in the following (Figure 6-27):
Evaluation Copy


                                 Figure 6-27 Collective Primitives and Block Collectives Relationships

             6.5 Reproducibility

             To produce repeatable results for Block Collectives and Collective Primitives, the same data
             elements from the Ranks in the Collective must be processed in the same order from one run to the
             next (this includes utilizing the same Stochastic Seeds for the different Collective Primitives or
             Block Collectives where Stochastic Rounding is used). This is necessary because some of the
             reduction operations for Collectives are not associative (e.g., for floating point addition, (a+b)+c is
             not necessarily equal to a+(b+c)).
             Achieving Reproducibility in UALink is a cooperative property of at least the application program
             design, the placement of the threads for the workload on various Accelerators, the interconnection
             between the Accelerators and Switches, and the design of the Switches and the Accelerators.
             UALink systems shall produce repeatable results from run to run on the exact same system for the
             same program whose threads of execution are allocated to the same Accelerators and are presented
             with the same initial input data if the workload does not contain any inherent non-determinism.
             One set of conditions to cause the Collective Primitives and Block Collectives to be processed
             against the same Rank data in the same order is listed below:
                  •   The program cannot contain any inherent non-determinism that would alter the run-to-run
                      behavior of the workload when presented with the same initial data with the threads of
                      execution executed in the same manner on the same Accelerators assuming the In Network
                      Collectives inherently maintain the same reduction order across the software ranks.

                                                   UALink In-Network Collectives                                 180

                       © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                      Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                          Rev 2.0

                  •   All Accelerators should issue Requests for the same set of Address to the same Egress Ports,
                      and this allocation should be fixed.
Evaluation Copy
                  •   All Switches should perform reductions for ReadReduce, REDUCE, or ALL-REDUCE in the
                      same fixed order. This order may be a linear order or partial order, and it may be in terms of
                      Accelerator IDs or Ports on the Switch.
                  •   All Switches should connect to a given Accelerator on the same port.
             Other sets of conditions may be possible if they ensure that the Collectives are processed against
             the same data in the same rank order given the same workload thread placement and initial data.
             Replication of a workload on a different system or configuration is not guaranteed. To replicate the
             execution of a program on a different system, steps must be taken to ensure that reduction order of
             the Ranks on the new system is consistent with the reduction order of the Ranks on the old system.
             To provide Reproducibility, the system should provide an implementation specific means to
             determine the reduction order or partial order for Collectives executed on a given set of
             Accelerators in the system. This means should be employed on the first run on the initial system to
             determine the reduction order of the Accelerators on that system and by implication the reduction
             order of the workload threads as assigned to those Accelerators on the initial system.
             To reproduce the run on a subsequent new system on a newly assigned set of Accelerators, that
             same system specific means should be applied to the new set of assigned Accelerators to determine
             the reduction order on the new system. The workload threads should then be assigned to the
             Accelerators on the new system in a manner that ensures the reduction order is the same on the
             new system as it was on the prior system.
             If the reduction order is a linear order, it will always be possible to permute the workload threads
             in a manner that achieves the same reduction order. However, if the reduction order is partial (say
             due to a hierarchical reduction of operands in the Switch), it may not be possible to find an
             assignment of workload threads on the new system that has the same reduction order as the old
             system.
             If an Accelerator should fail during an execution, to continue to be able to achieve Reproducibility,
             the threads of the workload should be reallocated to the set of Accelerators the program continues
             execution on in a way that preserves the reduction order for the Collectives (i.e. the Accelerator
             removal case is simply a special case of migrating the application to a new system).

             6.6 Rounding Modes and Stochastic Rounding

             UALink supports the following IEEE Rounding Modes:
                  •   (RNE) Round to nearest, ties to even
                  •   (RNA) Round to nearest, ties away from zero
                  •   (RZ) Round towards zero (truncation)
                  •   (RU) Round towards +infinity (rounding up or ceiling)
                  •   (RD) Round towards -infinity (round down or floor)
             With RNE being required and the other modes being optional. If the other modes are not
             implemented, they shall instead Round to Nearest, ties to even.

                                                   UALink In-Network Collectives                                181

                       © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             In addition to the IEEE Rounding modes, UALink supports an implementation defined Stochastic
             Rounding Mode. Stochastic Rounding generally consists of a random number source (typically a
Evaluation Copy
             Linear Feedback Shift Register) that generates a sequence of Random Numbers based on a Seed
             value. Stochastic Rounding is typically only applied when converting a higher precision floating
             point format down to a lower precision floating point format (and typically only applied when
             converting from a higher precision floating point format back to an FP8 format – E5M2 or E4M3).
             Stochastic Rounding typically involves taking a generated Random Number and adding that value
             to a set of Mantissa bits that are to be dropped in the down conversion and using the carry into the
             remaining retained mantissa bits to produce a “random” rounding.
             A Block Collective or Collective Primitive when presented with the same input data in the same
             reduction order for the Collective and the same Stochastic Seed shall produce consistent answers
             and rounding results.

             6.7 In Network Collective Optionality

             A Switch shall implement neither Collective Primitives nor Block Collectives, Collective Primitives
             only, or both Block Collectives and Collective Primitives. If Collective Primitives are implemented,
             the Switch shall implement all Collective Primitive Request types (ReadReduce, WriteMulticast,
             WriteFullMulticast, AtomicNRMulticast) and shall implement all reduction operations/datatypes
             for ReadReduce. If Block Collectives are implemented, the Switch shall implement all Block
             Collectives (REDUCE, ALL-REDUCE, BROADCAST) and shall implement all reduction
             operations/datatypes for REDUCE and ALL-REDUCE.




                                                  UALink In-Network Collectives                                182

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                       Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                           Rev 2.0

             7 Manageability Requirements
Evaluation Copy
             A UALink Pod is comprised of one or more UALink Accelerators connected via the UALink fabric,
             and managed by a UALink Pod Controller. Each Accelerator is hosted on a UALink System Node, and
             Accelerator traffic may be routed through the UALink fabric via UALink Switches. Multiple UALink
             Pods may be connected to create even larger Accelerator clusters; however, inter-Pod
             communication is outside the scope of this specification. While a brief overview of these
             components and their management interfaces is provided below, the full solution is documented in
             the separate Ultra Accelerator Link Manageability Specification.

             7.1 UALink Accelerators and System Nodes
             UALink System Nodes shall contain at least one UALink Accelerator, at least one high-performance
             host CPU to schedule and launch compute jobs, and at least one network interface. Commonly, a
             System Node should also include a baseboard management controller (BMC) for chassis
             management tasks and an interface for out-of-band management. The System Node acts as an OS
             Domain in that a single instance of an operating system or hypervisor may utilize all Accelerators in
             the System Node, or assign them to tenant Virtual Machines (VMs).
             Each System Node shall host one or more Node Management Agents that communicate with the
             Pod Controller as specified in the Ultra Accelerator Link Manageability Specification. Node
             Management Agents perform fabric management functions on the System Node such as adding
             Accelerator(s) on the System Node to a Virtual Pod by configuring the UALink ports. In the case of
             SR-IOV enabled Accelerators, the Node Management Agents shall only communicate with the
             Physical Function (PF) drivers that run in the host OS domain and are outside the trust boundary of
             any confidential VM.
             In aggregate across the Pod, the System Nodes may host up to 1024 Accelerators. Accelerators
             targeted for a Confidential Compute1 use case must support a minimum baseline of security
             features.
             The following figure illustrates one possible UALink System Node with a Node Management agent
             managing four UALink Accelerators (each with three ports). Also present are two host CPUs, a NIC,
             and a BMC.

                 https://confidentialcomputing.io/

             7.2 UALink Switches and Switch Platforms
             UALink Switches are responsible for routing traffic between Accelerators, and are managed by
             software/firmware called a Switch Management Agent. Physical Switches are implemented in
             hardware, but may be partitioned into multiple (Logical) Switches for purposes of bandwidth
             aggregation.
             Often, Physical Switches are hosted on Switch Platforms that run the Switch Management Agent on
             a processor (such as an x86 CPU or a Baseboard Management Controller). The processor is attached
             to each Physical Switch via a high-speed interface such as PCIe, as well as to a network interface for
             communication with the Pod Controller. Commonly, for security and identity purposes, a Switch
             Platform contains one or more e/iRoTs (Root of Trust) and/or a trusted platform module (TPM). A
             Switch Platform may contain a separate BMC for platform management.
             The Switch Management Agent shall interface with the Switches as specified in the Ultra Accelerator
             Link Manageability Specification. Detailed requirements for UALink Switches are included in the
             Switch Requirements chapter of this specification.
             The following figure illustrates one possible UALink Switch Platform with a Switch Management
             Agent running on a processor, managing two Physical Switches each with six ports. In this
             implementation, the processor and Physical Switches are separate devices. This Switch Platform
             also contains a NIC, a TPM, and a BMC separate from the processor.

                                                                 UALink Switch Platform
                                                           NIC              Switch Mgmt
                                                                               Agent           BMC
                                                           TPM              Processor
                                                                 Switch                   Switch




                                                         Figure 7-2. A UALink Switch Platform


             7.3 UALink Pod Controller
             The UALink Pod Controller software shall act as the centralized manager of the Pod’s resources, and
             should run external to the System Nodes and Switch Platforms under its management. Its
             responsibilities include:

                                                            Manageability Requirements                         184

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                       Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                           Rev 2.0

                   •   Pod resource configuration
                   •   Validation of Pod wiring against UALink rules and user-defined policies
Evaluation Copy
                   •   Allocation and assignment of Accelerator IDs
                   •   Setup and teardown for Switch routing
                   •   Managing Pod RAS including error recovery and log collection
                   •   Pod health management
                   •   Pod telemetry collection
             The Pod Controller interacts with several interfaces relating to the Pod:
                   •   It connects with each Node Management Agent to assign Accelerator IDs.
                   •   It connects with each Switch Management Agent to configure ports and set up routing
                       information for the Pod.
                   •   It may connect to each Switch Platform or System Node’s BMC to do out-of-band
                       management such as via Redfish2.
                   •   It may present one or more Pod-external interfaces such as a REST API, GUI, or command
                       terminal.
             A singular Pod Controller manages the Pod at any given time. However, implementations should
             consider Pod Controller architectures that permit high availability/redundancy/failover.
