             8.6 Security during collective operations
             The UALink security allows protecting tenant data during collective operation from the adversaries
             defined in the security model in Section 8.3. This is an optional feature that can be enabled if a
             tenant that wants to perform confidential computing uses collectives.
             The INC capable switch is assumed to be TEE capable and shall provide an interface to the trusted
             software on the node for secure configuration and management of tenant’s Virtual Pod Partition in
             the switch. A Virtual Pod Partition in the switch refers to all resources assigned to the Virtual Pod
             and all configurations associated with the Virtual Pod in the switch. These include switch ports,
             routing tables, INC primitives, internal memory, debug and performance monitoring settings, and
             other components of a Virtual Pod in the switch.
             Figure 8-13 shows the key elements of the security architecture for protecting tenant data during
             collective operation:
                  •   A TEE capable switch that has Switch Security Manager (SSM) and a SPDM responder
                      running on the security FW on the physical switch.
                                                                Security                                            236

                       © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                      Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                          Rev 2.0

                  •   An SPDM session between the TEE Security Manager on one of the nodes in the Virtual Pod
                      and the SSM. Only one of the nodes, designated as the lead node in the Virtual Pod, shall
Evaluation Copy
                      establish an SPDM session with SSM on the switch and will perform security management of
                      the Virtual Pod Partition on the switch. The designated lead node is assumed to perform the
                      following tasks as part of bringing switch into the TCB and enforcing Virtual Pod Partition
                      security.
                          o Attest the switch to verify its authenticity and integrity.
                          o Send TDISP equivalent commands such as LOCK, START and STOP/UNLOCK to
                              transition the Virtual Pod Partition to different states.
                          o Read Virtual Pod Partition configuration settings such as Virtual Pod’s routing table
                              to verify it against known good values.
                          o Verify the security policy of Virtual Pod Partition to verify that debug is disabled,
                              performance monitoring is off or restricted and other security settings are as
                              expected.
                      Since a switch can be configured to support multiple Virtual Pods, SSM is required to
                      support multiple SPDM sessions concurrently. The maximum number of concurrent SPDM
                      sessions supported by the switch determines the number of concurrent confidential Virtual
                      Pods (that use collectives) that can run.
                      The communication between trusted SW on the node and SSM can occur over UART
                      interface between GPU and switch as defined in base specification. In an alternate
                      implementation it may use ethernet channel such as the one used by Pod Controller to
                      communicate with the switch management SW. In the latter case, the untrusted SW on the
                      processor on the switch platform is required to forward the SPDM messages to SSM where
                      the crypto operations shall be performed. UALSec does not dictate use of specific
                      communication mechanism, however it does require that the SPDM crypto operations are
                      performed in the security FW on the switch ASIC.
                  •   A Crypto engine (CE) at the switch port decrypts the incoming collective request or
                      response at the ingress and encrypts the outgoing collective request or response at the
                      egress. The switch shall determine whether the incoming traffic is collective or non-
                      collective traffic (meant for another accelerator) by looking at the command encoding or the
                      tags and only decrypt the collective traffic. The non-collective traffic shall bypass the crypto
                      engine and remain encrypted inside the switch while being routed to the destination
                      accelerator. Likewise, only the outgoing collective traffic shall be encrypted at the egress,
                      and the non-collective traffic shall bypass the crypto engine on the way out and will retain
                      the end-to-end encryption between the accelerator ports. The accelerator-switch
                      encryption keys are selected by the trusted software and sent to SSM over the SPDM
                      session. The SSM shall program the encryption keys into the crypto engine of each switch
                      port in the Virtual Pod Partition.
                  •   The GroupTable in the switch, which is used by INC primitive to look up the group
                      membership information when performing collective operations, is assumed to be
                      programmed securely by the TVMs. The programming may happen via Load/Store over
                      UALink after UALink protection between accelerator and switch has been enabled, in which
                      case it would use the UALSec to protect GroupTable programming. If it is programmed by
                      an untrusted entity such as Pod Controller, it must be verified by the TVM before use.
                  •   The Virtual Pod’s Routing Table is expected to be programmed by the Pod Controller. At the
                      time the Virtual Pod Partition is transitioned to LOCK state, the Virtual Pod’s Routing Table
                      entry shall be locked and prevented from modification. The TVM is required to verify that
                                                              Security                                            237

                       © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                        Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                            Rev 2.0

                         the accelerator ID-port ID mappings in the routing table are programmed correctly before
                         transitioning the Virtual Pod Partition to RUN state via the TDISP equivalent START
Evaluation Copy
                         interface supported by SSM. In an example implementation the lead TVM, which already
                         collects accelerator ID and port numbers for all accelerators via the TVMs on their
                         respective nodes, gets the routing table information from the switch and verifies it matches
                         what is reported by the TVMs.

             8.6.1 Switch Port Identifier
             Each switch port in a Pod shall be uniquely identifiable via (DevID/DevType/PortNum) same as an
             accelerator port. UALSec uses the device ID and port number to construct the Context value used in
             key derivation function in order to derive port specific unique link encryption key as described in
             Section 8.6.4.2.
             8.6.1.1 Switch ID
             While Accelerator ID serves as the device ID for the accelerators in the Pod, switch devices are
             identified by a Switch ID. Just like the accelerators, the switches are assigned a 10-bit switch ID by
             the Pod Controller which serves as its device ID in DL messages and neighbor discovery and is used
             in UALSec for deriving the unique TX and RX keys for encrypting collective transactions between
             accelerator and switch.
             Accelerator IDs and Switch IDs have separate namespace, meaning they can have same IDs,
             however, within its own namespace the ID shall be unique. No two switches shall have the same
             switch ID, just as no two accelerators shall have the same accelerator ID. The lead TVM shall verify
                                                                                 Security                                                                           238

                          © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                   Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                       Rev 2.0

             the uniqueness of the switch IDs within its Virtual Pod same as how it verifies the uniqueness of
             accelerator ID within its Virtual Pod. TVM can get the switch ID from the switches over secure
Evaluation Copy
             SPDM session and verify that they are unique across the Virtual Pod during configuration and
             verification flow described in Section 8.6.6.
             8.6.1.2 Port Number
             UALink architecture assumes that the accelerators and switch ASICs come up with their own port
             numbering. The Pod Controller receives that numbering when the accelerators and switches
             register with the Pod Controller. The port number, which is a 10-bit value to allow switches to
             support 1024 accelerators in a physical Pod, is unique within a switch and is verified by the TVM.
             8.6.1.3 Neighbor programming, verification and locking
             The Pod Controller programs each port on the accelerators and switches with neighbor port’s
             (Device ID, Device Type, and Port Num) where the neighbor is the device on the other end of the
             cable. In a switched topology, the neighbor of any accelerator port is expected to be a switch port.
             Conversely, neighbor of an INC switch port is expected to be an accelerator in the current version of
             the UALink spec which supports only single layer INC switches.
             Thus, each accelerator port is programmed with switch ID and PortNum of the switch port it is
             attached to. And each switch port is programmed with accelerator ID and accelerator port num of
             the accelerator Port it is connected to.
             During port bring up, every port shall discover what DevID/DevType/PortNum is actually on the
             other side of the link ("Operational Neighbor") using the DL messages defined in the DL/PL
             specification and verify that the programming by the Pod Controller matches the physical topology.
             If it does not match, the link shall be prevented from progressing beyond the Data Link Layer (no
             TL/UPLI traffic), which means there can be no tenant traffic.
             When TVM applies TDISP LOCK on the accelerator to lock TDI configuration and equivalent LOCK
             on the switch to lock Virtual Pod Partition configuration during the setup process, the neighbor
             information programmed by the Pod Controller is locked on both on the accelerator and the switch
             and cannot be modified until the Virtual Pod Partition goes to UNLOCK state.
             8.6.1.4 Verifying and locking SrcAccID and destAccID at Ingress and Egress
             Each switch port has a SrcAccID register at ingress and DestAccID at egress that is programmed by
             the Pod Controller. Switch Security Manager must enforce that these match the device ID
             programmed as part of neighbor information programmed at each port.
             When the Virtual Pod transitions to LOCK state, these registers shall be locked and prevented from
             being modified until Virtual Pod Partition transitions to UNLOCK state when the workload
             terminates or the Virtual Pod Partition goes into an error state.

             8.6.2 Encryption and authentication scheme for Accelerator-Switch link
             A tenant that has UALink security enabled can optionally enable link protection between the
             accelerator port and switch ports to protect tenant's collective data over the network.
             The current UALinkSec standard uses the exact same encryption and authentication scheme for
             protecting accelerator-switch link as the one used for end-to-end link protection between
             accelerators as described in Section 8.5. It uses the standard AES-GCM with crypto processing
             performed in the protocol/functional layer on the accelerator and the switch ports. It is thus just an
             instantiation of end-to-end encryption protocol defined between accelerator ports with the
             difference being that one of the endpoints is the directly attached switch port.
                                                            Security                                             239

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                         Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                             Rev 2.0

             However, device specific performance characteristics must be considered during implementation.
             For example, if the accelerator as a TX initiates key rolling, the timeouts for response to key roll
Evaluation Copy
             request may need to account for performance differences in key derivation on the switch ports.
             Figure 8-14 shows an example system with two Virtual Pods with Accelerator-Switch link
             protection enabled for both the Virtual Pods. The accelerator and switch ports for the two virtual
             pods are programmed with separate set of master keys providing cryptographic isolation between
             the two mutually untrusted Virtual Pods.

                    CE                   CE                          CE                CE                                 CE                  CE                            CE               CE




                  Port 0                       Port N          Port N+1                     Port M                   Port 0                         Port N           Port N+1                   Port M
                  CE                          CE                CE                          CE                       CE                             CE                CE                        CE




                  Group                       Group                Group                Group                        Group                         Group                 Group                Group
                  Table                       Table                Table                Table                        Table                         Table                 Table                Table

                Primitive                 Primitive           Primitive                Primitive     Routing     Primitive                     Primitive            Primitive                Primitive       Routing
                 Engine                    Engine              Engine                   Engine                    Engine                        Engine               Engine                   Engine

             8.6.3 Ordering Requirements
             The current UALink spec (2.0) supports only single-level collectives with collectives supported only
             in the leaves. The collective transactions are assumed to maintain ordering between a given source
             and destination pair and within the same stream type.
             There are no ordering requirements on collective transactions between different source-
             destination pairs and between different stream types.

             8.6.4 Encryption Keys and Security State Management
             There is a single INC active master key per Virtual Pod for deriving TX and RX keys for accelerator-
             switch links. The {accl, accl}MasterKey != {accl, switch}MasterKey and the trusted software is required to
             select unique set of master keys (primary and alternate) for accelerator-accelerator links and for
             accelerator-switch links. The accl-switch master keys are used to derive TX and RX keys for
             encrypting accl-switch traffic.
             There are two INC master keys, a primary and an alternate key that are programmed by the TVMs
             into each accelerator and switch over secure SPDM session.
             While the software implementation of how the master keys are distributed and programmed is out
             of scope of this UALink specification, an example implementation might be where a lead TVM
             selects the INC master keys and distributes it securely to TVMs on the other nodes in the Virtual
                                                                                                          Security                                                                                                 240

                            © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                       Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                           Rev 2.0

             Pod. Each TVM would then program the INC master key in their local accelerators over TDISP
             enabled SPDM session. The Device Security Manager (DSM) in each of the accelerator would then
Evaluation Copy
             distribute the master key over secure HW channel to each port in the accelerator.
             The same lead TVM would then distribute the INC master keys to the SSM in each of the switch over
             SPDM session between the lead TVM and the switch. The SSM in turn would then program the
             master keys into each of the switch ports that is assigned to the Virtual Pod.
             The key management policy for INC keys is same as the one for the accelerator-to-accelerator
             encryption keys as described in Section 8.5.7.1.1.
             8.6.4.1 Key States
             Each accelerator port is attached to only 1 switch port and each switch port is attached to only one
             accelerator port. Therefore, each accelerator port has the following additional keys for accelerator
             to switch encryption/decryption.
                  1. 1 set of TX key material for encrypting collective transaction sent from the accelerator to
                     the switch port it is attached to.
                  2. 1 set of RX key material for decrypting collective transaction received from the switch port
                     it is attached to.
             And each switch port has:
                  1. 1 set of TX key material for encrypting collective transactions sent from the switch to the
                     accelerator port it is attached to
                  2. 1 set of RX key material for decrypting collective transactions received from the accelerator
                     port it is attached to.
             More specifically, each accelerator port and switch port will have the following key material for
             collective transactions, in addition to the key material described in Section 8.5.7.1.1 for accelerator-
             to-accelerator encryption.
                  1.   1 active INC Master Key (32B)
                  2.   1 alternate INC Master Key (32B)
                  3.   1 Master key usage counter (4B)
             It will have the following TX key material for encrypting and authenticating collective requests:
                  1.   1 Current TX derived key (32B)
                  2.   1 Next TX derived key (32B)
                  3.   1 Invocation field for stream 0 (4B)
                  4.   1 Invocation field for stream 1 (4B)
                  5.   1 Invocation field for stream 2 (4B)
                  6.   1 Epoch counter (4B)
                  It will have the following RX key material for decrypting and verifying collective response:
                  1.   1 Current RX derived key (32B)
                  2.   1 Next RX derived key (32B)
                  3.   1 Invocation field for stream 0 (4B)
                  4.   1 Invocation field for stream 1 (4B)
                  5.   1 Invocation field for stream 2 (4B)
                  6.   1 Epoch counter (4B)

                      Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification

             8.6.4.2 Key derivation for accelerator-switch link protection
             The same KDF function as defined in Section 8.5.7.2 is used to derive the TX and RX encryption keys

             for collective traffic. However, since the endpoints are not symmetrical the Context value, which is
             an input to the KDF, is constructed using accelerator and switch device IDs as shown in the Table
             8-27 and Table 8-28. In a switch-accelerator link, the context value includes 2-bits to identify the
             device pair type: 2b’01 for source accelerator – destination switch in Table 8-27, and 2b’10 for
             source switch – destination accelerator in Table 8-28.

             IV construction for accelerator-switch link protection is described in Section 8.5.7.3.

             8.6.5 Detecting collective traffic
             The switch port needs to be able to detect if the incoming transaction is a collective transaction in
             order to decide whether it needs to be decrypted or it should bypass the crypto engine. Likewise,
             the accelerator needs to be able to detect which incoming transaction is a collective transaction in
             order to decide if it needs to use the switch key material to authenticate and decrypt it. The
             command encoding or tag is used to detect collective transactions depending on the requester and
             responder.
                  •   A collective request from accelerator to switch has the collective command encoded in the
                      ReqCmd field. Switch uses that to detect the incoming collective request. The
                      ReqDstPhysAccID field is overloaded with a GroupID[9:0] value. However, the switch port
                      already knows the requesting accelerator ID from the SrcAccID register at the ingress and
                      has only one RX key (since a switch port is attached only one accelerator port) and does not
                      need to deal with key selection.
                  •   In response to a collective request received from an originating accelerator, the switch
                      routes the collective request to destination accelerators. The switch uses the same ReqCmd
                      encoding to issue a collective command to the Destination Accelerators as the one received
                                                               Security                                         242

                       © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                       Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                           Rev 2.0

                       from the Requesting Accelerator allowing the Destination Accelerator to distinguish the
                       collective request and use the switch key material for authentication and decryption.
Evaluation Copy
                  •    The Destination Accelerator Completer retains indications (tags) for whether the Request
                       was a Collective Primitive (ReadReduce, WriteMulticast, WriteFullMulticast,
                       AtomicNRMultiast), a Block Collective (BlockWriteFull, BlockRead), or neither of the above.
                       The Completer uses the indications saved above to set the RdRspTypeInfo or
                       WrRspTypeInfo fields in the Response. The switch port uses RdRspTypeInfo or
                       WrRspTypeInfo fields in the Response to determine if it is a collective response and needs
                       to be authenticated and decrypted at the ingress.
                  •    After the INC primitive has received all the response, it will collate or reduce the response
                       as needed and send the final response back to the originating accelerator. The switch uses
                       the original command encoding in the final response, which indicates to the originating
                       accelerator that the response is a collective response and must be authenticated and
                       decrypted using the switch key material.

             8.6.6 Configuring and starting Accelerator-Switch link protection
             The UALink protection for collectives is enabled on a per Virtual Pod basis. When the accelerator-
             switch link protection is enabled, all collective traffic to and from accelerator ports to the neighbor
             switch ports in the Virtual Pod shall be encrypted and authenticated. All accelerator ports in the
             Virtual Pod and their neighbor switch ports, shall have security enabled and shall be programmed
             with the same INC master keys and key rotation and key swap thresholds. They shall all derive the
             TX and RX keys per the key derivation function described in Section 8.6.4.2. If link protection
             between accelerator and switch has been enabled, the Virtual Pod Partition in the switch shall not
             send or receive any tenant transaction until the Virtual Pod Partition has been transitioned to RUN
             state via TDISP equivalent START interface.
             The trusted software that uses link protection for collectives is required to enable and configure
             both end-to-end link protection between the accelerators and accelerator-switch link protection
             upfront before starting workload execution. Once the accelerators are in the TDISP RUN state, link
             protection between accelerator and switch cannot be enabled without transitioning the accelerator
             to a STOP state via TDISP STOP interface which would require halting the workload execution.
             An example workflow for configuring and starting both end-to-end to link protection and
             accelerator-switch link protection is described as follows:
                  1. Under the control of a lead TVM, all TVMs in the Virtual Pod enable and configure all
                     accelerators in the Virtual Pod for end-to-end link protection per steps described in Section
                     8.5.8. However, lead TVM does not tell other TVMs to transition the accelerator to RUN state
                     yet. Which means they do not begin to transmit or receive tenant transactions.
                  2. The lead TVM sets up SPDM session with SSM of all switches in the Virtual Pod and verifies
                     their attestation report.
                  3. The lead TVM then transitions the Virtual Pod Partition in each switch to LOCK state via
                     TDISP equivalent LOCK interface supported by SSM.
                  4. The lead TVM collects the neighbor port information from all TVMs, gets the status of
                     Virtual Pod Partition from the switch and verifies:
                         a. Switch IDs and switch port numbers are unique
                         b. The routing table mappings for the Virtual Pod reported by the switch match the
                             neighbor port mapping collected from the TVMs.

                                                               Security                                           243

                        © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                       Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                           Rev 2.0

                         c. Security settings such as debug state and performance monitoring are configured as
                             expected.
Evaluation Copy
                  5. The lead TVM selects primary and alternate INC master keys and other global key material
                     and
                         a. Distributes the INC key material to all TVMs which program them in the accelerator
                             ports.
                         b. Programs the INC key material in the switches via their respective SPDM sessions.
                  6. The lead TVM transitions the Virtual Pod to RUN state:
                         a. Sends SPDM message to the switches to transition the Virtual Pod Partition to RUN
                             state
                         b. Tells all TVMs to transition their accelerators to RUN state.
                  7. After all of this has been successfully completed successfully, the lead TVM initiates secure
                     workload execution.

             8.6.7 Handling integrity failure in Accelerator-Switch link
             If either accelerator or switch detect an integrity failure during authentication tag verification, it
             shall drop the failing transaction and stop processing new transactions (all streams) from that
             source, thus stalling the pipeline which will eventually result in timeout. Only the port that found
             the failure will take this action. Additionally, the associated transmitter on the same port shall stop
             sending any transaction to that neighbor.
             If the failure is detected in the accelerator port, it shall isolate the neighbor switch and notify the
             accelerator security processor using a mechanism that is secure from tampering by the adversaries
             defined in the security model. The security processor is required to notify the software allowing
             software to take appropriate action for handling the failure.
             If the failure is detected in the switch port, it shall isolate the neighbor accelerator and notify the
             SSM. The SSM shall report this failure to the Pod Controller software which is required to notify the
             TVM via the Node Agent. Note, the Pod Controller is not trusted to guarantee the delivery of failure
             notification to TVM. However, failing to inform the TVM prevents the TVM from handling the failure
             gracefully and can only result in denial of service attack which is outside the security scope of
             UALSec.
             Failure in one of the accelerator-switch link does not cause other links to fail. The other links will
             continue to operate normally, though it is expected that the SW would likely need to stop and reset
             the Virtual Pod as the failure in one link may result in cascading failure across the workload
             execution,


             8.7 Keyrollmsg formats
             1.5 version of the specification modifies the Keyrollmsg in the following way:
                  1. The Keyrollmsg is used to trigger a key derivation in an accelerator endpoint if Attr[0] == 0
                     and Attr[1] == 0 .
                  2. The keyrollmsg triggers a key derivation and a key swap in an accelerator endpoint if
                     Attr[0] == 1 and Attr[1] == 0
                  3. The Keyrollmsg is used to trigger a key derivation in an INC capable switch that supports
                     INC security if Attr[0] == 0 and Attr[1] == 1 .
                  4. The keyrollmsg triggers a key derivation and a key swap in an INC capable switch that
                     support INC security if Attr[0] == 1 and Attr[1] == 1
                                                             Security                                            244

                        © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                    Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                        Rev 2.0

             Switches shall parse Keyrollmsg request only if it supports INC and INC security is enabled
             otherwise it shall forward it to the destination that is specified in the destination ID field.
Evaluation Copy
             If a requestor sends a keyrollmsg with Attr[1] = 1 to an accelerator Endpoint, the receiving
             endpoint shall drop it and raise an error event to the security processor in that accelerator.

             8.8 Specification version compatibility
             There is no expectation that a 2.0 compliant accelerator or switch needs to be compatible with 1.0
             for security features. An implementation is permitted to be only 2.0 compliant or 1.0 compliant or
             both.

             9 UALink Switch Requirements
Evaluation Copy
             9.1 Overview
             UALink Switches serve to relay UALink Requests and Responses between Accelerators.
             All switch ports shall connect to Accelerators and Switch-to-switch links shall not be supported.
             Each Request or Response shall be produced by a Source Accelerator, shall enter the Switch via an
             ingress Link, shall be relayed by the Switch to a single egress Link, where it shall consumed by a
             Destination Accelerator. All Response and Request relaying for traffic other than In Network
             Collective traffic shall be unicast.
             For traffic other than In Network Collective traffic, a UALink Switch is only responsible for the
             delivery of Requests and Responses between accelerators and shall not be required to decode the
             contents of these Requests or Responses beyond that necessary to deliver the Requests and
             Reponses, nor shall the Switch track any Request/Response state.
             Inbound DL flits arriving from the Source Accelerator shall be unpacked into TL flits, which shall
             then be further unpacked into individual Requests and Responses for forwarding between Ports.
             The Destination Accelerator ID field within each Request or Response shall index into a routing
             table to determine the egress Station and Port to which it must be forwarded. Once forwarded
             between ports, outbound Requests and Responses shall be packed into TL flits, which in turn shall
             be packed into DL flits and sent to the Destination Accelerator.
             Each Request or Response shall be routed independently. Multiple Requests or Responses
             unpacked from the same inbound TL flit do not necessarily route to the same egress Station and
             Port and multiple Requests and Responses packed into the same outbound TL may have arrived
             from multiple ingress stations and ports.
             The UALink Switch shall contain a complete implementation of the UALink DL and TL, similar to
             that found in an Accelerator. The nominal interface between TL and switch core shall be the UPLI
             Originator/Completer interface, although a Switch design may substitute any suitable vendor-
             defined interface. Any vendor-defined interface replacing UPLI shall mimic any UPLI mandated
             Originator or Completer behaviors in this Specification, including but not limited to UPLI Drop
             modes (see 3.1.3) and Port ID field manipulation (See 2.4).
             Flow control across an individual link between accelerator and switch, including the required
             independence of Requests vs Responses, shall be handled by the TL layer logic on each end of the
             link. Requests or Responses stalled by flow control shall wait within the outbound TL until they are
             eligible, at which time they may be included in a TL flit being packed and sent. Flow control for
             Requests or Responses being forwarded between Source and Destination Stations also respects the
             independence of Requests versus Responses, as does the UPLI interface between TL and switch
             core.

             9.2 Bifurcation support
             Link bifurcation feature allows a station to split into multiple independent ports. Each ULS station
             has four lanes. Each station shall support independent configured as a single four-lane port, two
             two-lane ports, or four single-lane ports.

             9.3 Lossless Request and Response delivery
             Except in error cases which explicitly require Requests or Responses to be dropped (see Section
             3.1.3), all Requests or Responses shall be relayed through the switch in a lossless manner. Link
                                                  UALink Switch Requirements                                    246

                     © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                      Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                          Rev 2.0

             level Retry (LLR) shall be used to ensure delivery from source Accelerator to the switch ingress
             port, and from switch egress port to destination Accelerator. Flow control shall ensure that
Evaluation Copy
             Requests and Responses are never dropped due to receive-buffer space limitations.

             9.4 Non-blocking architecture
             Traffic between any one pair of Ports shall operate independently of traffic between any other pair
             of Ports. However, switch core architecture may share resources across ports within a station or
             even amongst multiple stations.
             Ingress and egress traffic of a port shall operate independently of each other. Ingress Requests shall
             operate independently of ingress Responses, and egress Requests shall operate independently of
             egress Responses.
             Stalled egress Requests or Responses to one port of a station may block same station egress
             Requests or Responses headed towards another port of the same station. Similarly, stalled ingress
             Requests or Responses from one port of a station may block like ingress Requests or Responses
             from another port of the same station. Under no circumstances shall stalled Requests be permitted
             to block Responses.
             While such blocking is permitted, it should be minimized by switch designs to the extent reasonably
             possible. Non-blocking architecture is a crucial property of network switch design. Switches should
             incorporate sufficient buffering at the ingress and/or egress ports to prevent congestion and
             blocking.

             9.5 Forward progress guarantee
             All arbitration within the ULS shall be starvation-free, to avoid livelock or starvation cases. This
             shall include but is not limited to arbitration between ingress ports within a station, arbitration
             between ingress stations competing for access to an egress station, and arbitration between
             Requests and Responses for access to available TL flit sectors.
             Starvation-free arbitration may include simple round-robin, weighted and/or deficit round-robin,
             age-based oldest-first, etc.

             9.6 Ordering and Virtual Channels
             Each port-to-port path through ULS shall follow Request or Response delivery ordering and Virtual
             Channel handling as defined for UPLI (see 2.7.9). Support for Strict Ordering mode is mandatory,
             and support for non-Strict Ordering mode is optional. If non-Strict Ordering mode is supported, it
             shall be selected on a per-port basis, with identical settings being used on ingress and egress ports.

             9.7 Routing Table Structure
             Routing decisions shall be made by looking up an entry in a Route Table at each Port based on the
             10-bit Destination Accelerator ID of inbound Requests and Responses (Destination Accelerator IDs
             shall be numbered from 0 to n-1 where “n” is the maximum number of Accelerators a Switch may
             support). The number of entries in the table shall equal or exceed the maximum number of
             supported ports in the Switch with single-lane bifurcation. Each table entry shall contain the
             following information:
                  •   An indication of whether to allow or deny routing Requests or Responses received by the
                      port based on the Destination Accelerator ID of the Request or Response. The Deny setting
                      may be used to prevent routing Switches in a partitioned Physical Switch, and between
                      Virtual Pods. Upon Switch reset, the Deny setting shall apply to all table entries.

                                                   UALink Switch Requirements                                       247

                       © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                      Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                          Rev 2.0

                  •   An indication of the Egress Port on the Switch (in terms of the Station and Port) to which
                      the Request or Response shall be routed if allowed. For example, in a switch with 64
Evaluation Copy
                      stations, each bifurcatable into at most four ports, each route table entry shall supply a 6-bit
                      station number and a 2-bit port number.
             Where the table contains fewer than 1,024 entries, Requests or Responses whose Destination
             Accelerator ID value exceeds the size of the table shall be denied routing. Requests or Responses
             which are denied routing (either due to the allow/deny indication or exceeding the Route Table
             size) shall be silently dropped and shall be logged by the management controller.
             9.7.1 Routing Table Instances
             The switch shall contain a separate, independently programmable instance of the Route Table for
             each possible Port (due to bifurcation) per station (i.e. a station that can be bifurcated into four
             Ports shall have four independent Route Tables).
             The independent programmability of different routing table instances, and of the independent deny
             controls for different ports within each bi-furcated station, allow a switch to be subdivided into
             multiple independent Virtual Switches , serving multiple Virtual Pods.
             9.7.2 Egress port reachability
             All egress ports of all stations shall be reachable from any ingress port, with no arbitrary
             restrictions. This shall include allowing route-to-self (a.k.a. U-turn) cases, where the Requests and
             Responses ingress and egress via the same port.

             9.8 Configuration
             Configurability options are left up to the implementation. The list below is an incomplete but a
             useful list:
                  •   Accelerator ID value associated with each port.
                  •   Routing table associated with each port, as described in Section (see 9.7)Bifurcation mode
                      for each station.
                  •   For each port, the Strict versus non-Strict ordering mode (see 9.6).
                  •   For each port, whether or not Authentication is enabled, which affects TL flit packing and
                      unpacking.
                  •   Ability to determine which stations are in drop mode, and in the case of port-granular
                      modes, ability to determine which ports are in drop mode.
                  •   Ability to reset drop modes to resume normal operation, with station granular or port
                      granular control, as appropriate.

             9.9 UALink Switch Recommendations and Goals
             The following subsections are recommendations and goals and are not meant to form UALink
             switch requirements but are meant to help guide implementors in areas of importance.
             9.9.1 Debug
             It is recommended that UALink switches support both transaction injection with and without
             errors towards switch core and towards the UALink. Other vendor defined debug support is
             allowed, but none is required under this section. UALink switches are recommended to support
             injection of key errors (i.e. link errors, link down, at least one silicon ECC error) to allow for
             platform/system level testing of RAS. The Injection, if supported, shall be disabled by default during
             mission mode and shall have a mechanism to allow enabling injection through a secure FW switch.

                                                    UALink Switch Requirements                                    248

                       © 2025-2026 ULTRA ACCELERATOR LINK CONSORTIUM, INC. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
                      Ultra Accelerator Link Consortium Inc. (UALink) - UALink Common Specification
                                                          Rev 2.0

             9.9.2 Latency goals
             UALink Switches are recommended to target the following idle and unloaded pin-to-pin latency for
Evaluation Copy
             a 64 byte Write Request on a 4-lane non bifurcated port with FEC enabled based on size of the
             switch:
                  •   128 lane switch: <200ns
                  •   256 lane switch: <250ns
                  •   512 lane switch: <300ns

             9.9.3 Performance goals
             It is recommended that UALink Switch core should maintain a post-FEC line rate of
             200Gbps. Switches should be capable of maintaining line rate for pairwise communication as well
             as concurrent communication across a small group (8-to-32) of accelerators. DL and TL protocol
             overhead will lower the effective line rate based on the traffic pattern and security protocols.
